Then the solution $(w,\bar{\chi})(\cdot;k,c_r+\mathrm{i}\delta)$ with values $(0,\bar{A}_\delta(h_{\mathrm{a}})V_\delta(h_{\mathrm{a}}))$ at $z=h_{\mathrm{a}}$ converges as $\delta\downarrow0$, uniformly on $[0,h_{\mathrm{a}}]$ at rate $O\bigl(\delta(1+|\log(\delta/V_\ast)|)\bigr)$, to the unique solution $(w_\ast,\bar{\chi}_\ast)$ of the limit problem with the limiting values $(0,\bar{A}_0(h_{\mathrm{a}})V_0(h_{\mathrm{a}}))$ at the top boundary.
The limit $w_\ast$ is continuous on $[0,h_{\mathrm{a}}]$ and $C^1$ away from the critical levels, and at each $z_j$ its derivative has the logarithmic behavior and the jump in the renormalized derivative in Equations~\eqref{eq:renorm-deriv}--\eqref{eq:wjump} of \cref{prop:local}\ref{it:basis-log}.
The function $\bar{\chi}_\ast$ is Lipschitz on $[0,h_{\mathrm{a}}]$, and $(w_\ast,\bar{\chi}_\ast)(0)$, $w_\ast(z_j)$ depend $C^1$ on $c_r$.
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Partition $[0,h_{\mathrm{a}}]$ into the local intervals $I_j$ and the closed intervals between them that contain no critical level.
On the latter intervals the coefficients of Equation~\eqref{eq:wchi-recall} are $C^2$ uniformly in $\delta\in[0,\delta_1]$ and Lipschitz in $\delta$, so the solution maps are invertible linear maps that converge at rate $O(\delta)$ by Gr\"onwall's inequality and depend $C^1$ on $c_r$.
The values at the top boundary also depend on $\delta$, and $Y_\delta(h_{\mathrm{a}}):=(0,\bar{A}_\delta(h_{\mathrm{a}})V_\delta(h_{\mathrm{a}}))$ satisfies $Y_\delta(h_{\mathrm{a}})-Y_0(h_{\mathrm{a}})=O(\delta)$ by uniform subsonicity.
Since the solution is obtained from $Y_\delta(h_{\mathrm{a}})$ by composing finitely many uniformly bounded maps, as described below, this $O(\delta)$ difference does not change the stated rate.
Across each $I_j$, \cref{lem:fixedpoint} identifies the solution with a fixed point, whose constants $(\alpha,\beta)$ depend linearly and invertibly on the values at $z_j+\varsigma$, with bounds uniform in $\delta$ by \cref{prop:convergence}\ref{it:conv-matrices}.
The same proposition gives convergence of $T_\delta$ at rate $O(\delta\log(1/\delta))$, together with $C^1$-dependence on $c_r$.
Composing the finitely many maps from $h_{\mathrm{a}}$ down to $0$ proves the claims.
The interior description of $w_\ast$ is \cref{prop:local}\ref{it:basis-reg}--\ref{it:basis-log}.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:limabs}
Let $c_r$ be a regular value of $U$ satisfying Hypotheses \ref{hyp:critical}--\ref{hyp:nonres} with $c_k$ replaced by $c_r$.
Then the following hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item\label{it:limabs-Z} $Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,\cdot)$ extends continuously to the closed upper half-plane locally at $c_r$, that is, the limit
  \begin{equation}
  Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_r):=\lim_{\delta\downarrow0}Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c_r+\mathrm{i}\delta)
  \end{equation}
  exists, is finite, is attained at rate $O\bigl(\delta(1+|\log(\delta/V_\ast)|)\bigr)$, written $O(\delta\log(1/\delta))$ in the convention of \S\ref{subsec:local}, and is $C^1$ in $c_r$ on the set of regular values satisfying Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres}.
\item\label{it:limabs-flux} the limiting flux $\mathcal{J}_\ast=\mathcal{W}_\ast/k^2$ of the solution normalized by $\xi(0)=1$, that is, by $w_\ast(0)=-\mathrm{i} kc_r$, is piecewise constant, vanishes on $(z_m,h_{\mathrm{a}})$, and jumps at each critical level by
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:Jjump}
  \mathcal{J}_\ast(z_j^+)-\mathcal{J}_\ast(z_j^-)
  =\frac{\pi}{k^2}\,
  \frac{\bar{\rho}_0(z_j)\,Q(z_j)}{|U'(z_j)|}\;\bigl|w_\ast(z_j)\bigr|^2 .
  \end{equation}
\item\label{it:limabs-residue} consequently
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:residue}
  \operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_r)
  =\mathcal{J}_\ast(0)
  =-\frac{\pi}{k^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}
  \frac{\bar{\rho}_0(z_j)\,Q(z_j)}{|U'(z_j)|}\bigl|w_\ast(z_j)\bigr|^{2}
  =-\pi c_r^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}
  \frac{\bar{\rho}_0(z_j)\,Q(z_j)}{|U'(z_j)|}\bigl|\hat y(z_j)\bigr|^{2},
  \end{equation}
  where $\hat y:=w_\ast/w_\ast(0)$ is the interface-normalized limiting solution.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
For \ref{it:limabs-Z}, by Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres}, $w_\ast(0)\neq0$, so by \cref{prop:limit-solutions} $|w(0;k,c_r+\mathrm{i}\delta)|\ge \tfrac12|w_\ast(0)|$ for small $\delta$.
Equation~\eqref{eq:Za-formula} and the uniform convergence of $(w,\bar{\chi})(0)$ give existence and the rate, and together with the $C^1$-dependence of $(w_\ast,\bar{\chi}_\ast)(0)$ on $c_r$ from \cref{prop:limit-solutions} they give the smoothness in $c_r$.

For \ref{it:limabs-flux}, on each interval free of critical levels the limit solution solves a real-coefficient system, so $\mathcal{W}=\operatorname{Im}(\bar{A} w_\ast'w_\ast^{\ast})$ is constant there because $\mathcal{W}'=\operatorname{Im}\bigl((\bar{A} w')'w^{\ast}\bigr)+\operatorname{Im}\bigl(\bar{A}|w'|^2\bigr) =\operatorname{Im}\bigl((\bar{\rho}_0 k^2+G/V)|w|^2\bigr)+0=0$, all coefficients being real.
This is \cref{lem:flux}\ref{it:flux-real} combined with Equation~\eqref{eq:J-W}.
At the top boundary, $w_\ast(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$ gives $\mathcal{W}(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$, so $\mathcal{W}_\ast\equiv0$ on $(z_m,h_{\mathrm{a}})$.
The jumps are \cref{prop:local}\ref{it:quadratic}.
The normalization rescales the solution by a complex constant, which multiplies $\mathcal{W}$ and $u_1$ by the same positive factor, so Equation~\eqref{eq:Wjump} still holds after normalization, and $\mathcal{J}=\mathcal{W}/k^2$ by Equation~\eqref{eq:J-W}.

For \ref{it:limabs-residue}, write $[\mathcal{J}_\ast]_{z_j}:=\mathcal{J}_\ast(z_j^+)-\mathcal{J}_\ast(z_j^-)$ for the jump at $z_j$.
Since $\mathcal{J}_\ast$ vanishes on $(z_m,h_{\mathrm{a}})$ and is constant between critical levels, summing the jumps from $h_{\mathrm{a}}$ down to $0$ gives $\mathcal{J}_\ast(0)=-\sum_j[\mathcal{J}_\ast]_{z_j}$.
The relation $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^+=\operatorname{Im}\bar{q}^+(0)=\mathcal{J}_\ast(0)$, where $\bar{q}^+(0)$ is the limit of $\bar{q}(0)$ as $\delta\downarrow0$, holds because $\xi(0)=1$ is real and the hydrostatic term in Equation~\eqref{eq:Za-def} is real.
The final expression substitutes $|w_\ast(z_j)|^2=k^2c_r^2|\hat y(z_j)|^2$.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\label{rem:EP}
Equation~\eqref{eq:Jjump} gives the signed change of wave-momentum flux across each critical level.
When the sign condition for Miles growth holds, the flux is absorbed at the critical levels in proportion to the specific-vorticity gradient.
Equation~\eqref{eq:residue} shows that the air-pressure component in phase with the interface slope, equivalently shifted by a quarter wavelength from the interface displacement, is determined by the total critical-layer flux change.
This is the rigorous, compressible form of Miles's resonance argument \cite{Miles1957,Lighthill1962} and of the Eliassen--Palm theory, in which steady waves without dissipation leave the mean flow unchanged except where their momentum flux is absorbed, as at critical levels \cite{EliassenPalm1961,BookerBretherton1967,AndrewsMcIntyre1978}.
For $N^2=0$ there is no internal-gravity-wave radiation, so the flux changes only across the critical levels and vanishes above the highest one because $w(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
\label{rem:resonance}
The map $c_r\mapsto w_\ast(0;c_r)$ is $C^1$ on intervals of regular values with the same number of critical levels (\cref{prop:limit-solutions}).
Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} requires this trace to be nonzero.
It follows from \cref{lem:positivity} under the sign condition of \cref{cor:sufficient}, and it can otherwise be checked for a prescribed profile.
A zero of $w_\ast(0;c_r)$ means that the atmospheric boundary-value problem with the interface held at rest has a nontrivial solution at $c_r$.
When the equation for $w$ has solutions that oscillate in height, such zeros include resonances of the air layer between the interface held at rest and the top boundary, for example acoustic standing waves when the flow relative to the wave is supersonic.
In the uniform-wind example of \S\ref{subsec:KH}, the corresponding solutions in the uniformly subsonic regime are evanescent, that is, they do not oscillate in height, and they decay exponentially with height.
For a general wind profile, such solutions need not decay monotonically or exponentially with height.
Such a zero is a property of the limiting air problem alone.
\cref{prop:reduction} excludes coupled normal modes with $\hat\zeta=0$ only for $\operatorname{Im} c\neq0$, and we do not apply it at the real phase speed $c_r$.
At $c_r=c_k$, such a zero means that $c_k$ is a zero of both $D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,\cdot)$ and $w_\ast(0;\cdot)$, the limit of the second factor of $\mathfrak{E}(k,c,0)=D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c)\,w(0;k,c)$, as described in \cref{rem:determinant}.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
\label{prop:Zhalfdisc}
Assume Hypotheses \ref{hyp:reg}--\ref{hyp:nonres}.
There exist $r_0>0$ and $C,M_Z>0$ such that, with $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}:=\{c\in\mathbb{C}:\,|c-c_k|\le r_0,\ \operatorname{Im} c\ge0\}$, the following hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item\label{it:Z-nopole} With $m_0:=\min_{|c_r-c_k|\le r_0}|w_\ast(0;c_r)|>0$, every $c\in\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$ satisfies $|w(0;k,c)|\ge m_0/2$.
Hence $Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,\cdot)$ is holomorphic in the interior of $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$, extends continuously to $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$ with boundary value $Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}$ on the real interval $[c_k-r_0,c_k+r_0]$, and $|Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)|\le M_Z$ there.
\item\label{it:Z-modulus} For all $c\in\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$,
\begin{equation}
\bigl|Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)-Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)\bigr|\le\omega\bigl(|c-c_k|\bigr),
\qquad
\omega(r):=C\,r\bigl(1+\bigl|\log(r/V_\ast)\bigr|\bigr).
\end{equation}
\item\label{it:Z-deriv} For $c$ in the interior of $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$ with $|c-c_k|\le r_0/2$,
\begin{equation}
\bigl|\partial_cZ_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)\bigr|
\le C\bigl(1+\bigl|\log(\operatorname{Im} c/V_\ast)\bigr|\bigr).
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-9 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
For \ref{it:Z-nopole}, by Hypothesis \ref{hyp:critical} and the implicit function theorem there is $r_0>0$ such that every $c_r\in[c_k-r_0,c_k+r_0]$ is a regular value of $U$ with exactly $m$ critical levels $z_j(c_r)$ depending $C^1$ on $c_r$, and Hypotheses \ref{hyp:subsonic} and \ref{hyp:nonres} persist on this segment.
\cref{prop:limit-solutions} gives continuity of $w_\ast(0;\cdot)$, so $m_0>0$.
By \cref{prop:limit-solutions}, uniformly for $c_r$ in the segment, $|w(0;k,c_r+\mathrm{i}\delta)-w_\ast(0;c_r)|\le C\delta\bigl(1+|\log(\delta/V_\ast)|\bigr)$ for $\delta\in(0,\delta_1]$, where the uniformity in $c_r$ is part of \cref{lem:pullback}.
Shrinking $r_0$ so that $r_0\le\delta_1$ and $Cr_0\bigl(1+|\log(r_0/V_\ast)|\bigr)\le m_0/2$, every $c\in\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$ satisfies $|w(0;k,c)|\ge m_0/2$, since every such $c$ has $\operatorname{Im} c\le r_0\le\delta_1$.
Poles of $Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,\cdot)$ are zeros of $w(0;k,\cdot)$ (\cref{lem:regular-solve}), so $Z_{\mathrm{a}}$ is holomorphic in the interior of $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$.
The same uniform convergence of $(w,\bar{\chi})(0)$, together with the lower bound $|w(0;k,c)|\ge m_0/2$ for the denominator in Equation~\eqref{eq:Za-formula}, shows $|Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c_r+\mathrm{i}\delta)-Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_r)|\le C\delta\bigl(1+|\log(\delta/V_\ast)|\bigr)$ uniformly in $c_r$.
Since $Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}$ is $C^1$ on the segment by \cref{thm:limabs}\ref{it:limabs-Z}, this gives continuity on the compact set $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$, hence the bound $M_Z$.

For \ref{it:Z-modulus}, let $c=c_r+\mathrm{i}\delta\in\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$.
Then
\begin{align*}
\bigl|Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)-Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)\bigr|
&\le\bigl|Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)-Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_r)\bigr|
+\bigl|Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_r)-Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)\bigr|\\
&\le C\delta\bigl(1+|\log(\delta/V_\ast)|\bigr)+C|c_r-c_k| ,
\end{align*}
the second term by the uniform bound $|\partial_{c_r}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}|\le C$ from \cref{lem:pullback}.
Since $\delta\le|c-c_k|$, $|c_r-c_k|\le|c-c_k|$, and $r\mapsto r(1+|\log(r/V_\ast)|)$ is increasing on $(0,V_\ast]$, both terms are dominated by $\omega(|c-c_k|)$ after shrinking $r_0\le V_\ast$.

For \ref{it:Z-deriv}, let $c=c_r+\mathrm{i}\delta$ lie in the interior of $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$ with $|c-c_k|\le r_0/2$ and set $r_1:=\delta/2$, so the circle $|c'-c|=r_1$ lies in that interior ($r_1\le r_0/4$).
For $c'=c_r'+\mathrm{i}\delta'$ on that circle, $\delta'\in[\delta/2,3\delta/2]$ and $|c_r'-c_r|\le\delta/2$, so by the split used in \ref{it:Z-modulus},
\begin{align*}
|Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c')-Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)|
&\le C\delta'\bigl(1+|\log(\delta'/V_\ast)|\bigr)
+C|c_r'-c_r|
+C\delta\bigl(1+|\log(\delta/V_\ast)|\bigr)\\
&\le C'\delta\bigl(1+|\log(\delta/V_\ast)|\bigr).
\end{align*}
Cauchy's formula for the derivative, $\partial_cZ_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)=\frac1{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|c'-c|=r_1} \frac{Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c')-Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)}{(c'-c)^2}\,\,\mathrm{d} c'$, then gives $|\partial_cZ_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)|\le r_1^{-1}\sup_{|c'-c|=r_1}|Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c')-Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)| \le C\bigl(1+|\log(\delta/V_\ast)|\bigr)$.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:H-monotone}
On an interval free of critical levels define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:H-def}
H:=u_2-\frac{\bar{A}\,(U'+bV)}{V}\,u_1
\qquad(c=c_r\in\mathbb{R},\ V=U-c_r).
\end{equation}
Then along solutions of Equation~\eqref{eq:u-system-real},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:H-derivative}
\begin{aligned}
H'
&=\bar{\rho}_0 k^2u_1
+\frac{u_3}{\bar{A}}
+\bar{A}\Bigl(\frac{U'+bV}{V}\Bigr)^2u_1
-2\,\frac{U'+bV}{V}\,u_2\\
&\ge
\bar{\rho}_0 k^2u_1
+\Bigl(\sqrt{\tfrac{u_3}{\bar{A}}}
-\sqrt{\bar{A}}\,\Bigl|\tfrac{U'+bV}{V}\Bigr|\sqrt{u_1}\Bigr)^{2}
\;\ge\;\bar{\rho}_0 k^2 u_1\;\ge\;0.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Equality holds identically only for the trivial solution.
In particular $H$ is nondecreasing between critical levels.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
When we differentiate Equation~\eqref{eq:H-def} using Equation~\eqref{eq:u-system-real}, the singular and $b$-dependent terms cancel identically by the definition of $G$ in Equation~\eqref{eq:SL}, since
\begin{equation}
\frac GV-\Bigl(\frac{\bar{A}(U'+bV)}{V}\Bigr)'
=\frac{b\bar{A}(U'+bV)}{V}+\frac{\bar{A}(U'+bV)U'}{V^2},
\qquad
\frac{\bar{A}(U'+bV)}{V}\,u_1'
=\frac{2(U'+bV)}{V}u_2,
\end{equation}
and regrouping yields the displayed equality.
The inequality is Cauchy--Schwarz, $|u_2|\le\sqrt{u_1u_3}$ from Equation~\eqref{eq:conservation}, after completing the square.
Positivity of $\bar{A}$ uses Hypothesis \ref{hyp:subsonic}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:positivity}
Let $(w_\ast,\bar{\chi}_\ast)$ be the limit solution of \cref{prop:limit-solutions}, with $w(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$ and $\bar{\chi}(h_{\mathrm{a}})=\bar{A}(h_{\mathrm{a}})V(h_{\mathrm{a}})$, so that $u_1(h_{\mathrm{a}})=u_2(h_{\mathrm{a}})=\mathcal{W}(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$ and $u_3(h_{\mathrm{a}})>0$.
Then $u_1\ge0$ vanishes at most at isolated points, and $u_1(z_m)>0$.
If $m\ge2$ and $Q(z_m)\neq0$, then also $u_1(z_{m-1})>0$.
Moreover, if all $Q(z_j)$ have the same sign and $Q(z_m)\neq0$, then $u_1>0$ on $[0,z_m)$.
In particular $u_1(0)>0$, i.e. Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} holds automatically, and $\mathcal{W}(0)\neq0$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Consider the limiting solution at the real phase speed $c_r$.

We first show that $u_1$ vanishes at most at isolated points on any critical-level-free interval.
If $u_1(z^\flat)=0$ at an interior point $z^\flat$, then $w(z^\flat)=0$, and $w'(z^\flat)\neq0$, for otherwise $\bar{\chi}(z^\flat)=\bar{A}\bigl[Vw'-(U'+bV)w\bigr](z^\flat)=0$ as well and the solution would be trivial by uniqueness for the regular system in Equation~\eqref{eq:wchi-recall}.
Hence $z^\flat$ is an isolated zero of $w$.
If $w_\ast(z_j)=0$ at a critical level, \cref{prop:local}\ref{it:quadratic} shows that the nontrivial local solution is a nonzero multiple of the regular basis solution $w^{(1)}=VE\,(1+O(\varsigma))$.
Since $U'(z_j)\neq0$, $z_j$ is a simple, hence isolated, zero as well.

To prove $u_1(z_m)>0$, suppose $u_1(z_m)=0$.
By \cref{prop:local}\ref{it:quadratic}, on $I_m$ the solution is a complex multiple of the real regular solution $(w^{(1)},\bar{\chi}^{(1)})$.
The quantities $(u_1,u_2,u_3,\mathcal{W})$ extend continuously, and in fact $C^1$, across $z_m$, and $\mathcal{W}\equiv0$ on $I_m$.
The function $H$ of Equation~\eqref{eq:H-def} also extends continuously to $z_m$, because in $H=u_2-(\bar{A}(U'+bV)/V)\,u_1$ the pole of $1/V$ is canceled by $u_1=|\alpha w^{(1)}|^2=O((z-z_m)^2)$.
Since $u_2(z_m)=\operatorname{Re}\bigl(w^{[1]}w^{\ast}\bigr)(z_m)=0$ as well, we get $H(z_m)=0$.
On the other hand $H(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$, from $u_1(h_{\mathrm{a}})=u_2(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$, and by \cref{lem:H-monotone} together with the isolated-zeros property, $H(h_{\mathrm{a}})-H(z_m)\ge k^2\int_{z_m}^{h_{\mathrm{a}}}\bar{\rho}_0\,u_1>0$ for a nontrivial solution.
This contradicts $H(z_m)=H(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$.
So $u_1(z_m)>0$.

On $(z_m,h_{\mathrm{a}})$ the values at $h_{\mathrm{a}}$ and the coefficients are real, so the solution is real there and $\mathcal{W}\equiv0$ on $(z_m,h_{\mathrm{a}})$.
By Equation~\eqref{eq:Wjump},
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{W}\big|_{(z_{m-1},z_m)}
=\mathcal{W}(z_m^-)
=-\pi\,\frac{\bar{\rho}_0(z_m)Q(z_m)}{|U'(z_m)|}\,u_1(z_m)\;\neq\;0
\quad\text{when }Q(z_m)\neq0 ,
\end{equation}
using $\mathcal{W}'=0$ between levels, as in Equation~\eqref{eq:u-system-real}.
Since $u_1(z)=0$ forces $\mathcal{W}(z)=0$ at that point, as $\mathcal{W}=\operatorname{Im}(w^{[1]}w^{\ast})$, we get $u_1>0$ on all of $(z_{m-1},z_m)$.
Moreover $u_1(z_{m-1})=0$ is impossible because, by \cref{prop:local}\ref{it:quadratic} applied at $z_{m-1}$, it would make the solution locally a multiple of the real regular basis solution and hence $\mathcal{W}(z_{m-1}^+)=0$, contradicting $\mathcal{W}(z_{m-1}^+)=\mathcal{W}(z_m^-)\neq0$.
Thus $u_1(z_{m-1})>0$.

If all $Q(z_j)$ have the same sign and $Q(z_m)\neq0$, the jumps in Equation~\eqref{eq:Wjump} accumulate monotonically as $z$ decreases.
The flux $\mathcal{W}$ is constant between levels and $\mathcal{W}(z_j^-)=\mathcal{W}(z_j^+)-\pi(\bar{\rho}_0 Q/|U'|)(z_j)u_1(z_j)$ with every increment of the same sign (or zero), and the first increment, at $z_m$, is nonzero because $Q(z_m)\neq0$ and $u_1(z_m)>0$.
Hence $\mathcal{W}\neq0$ on $[0,z_m)\setminus\{z_j\}$, which as above forces $u_1>0$ there and at each $z_j$, $j<m$, and at $z=0$.
The inequality $u_1(0)>0$ is Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres}, and $\mathcal{W}(0)\neq0$.
When $Q(z_m)=0$ the flux vanishes identically on $(z_{m-1},h_{\mathrm{a}})$ and no conclusion about $u_1(z_{m-1})$ can be drawn from this argument.
This is why the assumption $Q(z_m)\neq0$ is needed here.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:coercive}
Assume Hypotheses \ref{hyp:reg}, \ref{hyp:noslip}, and \ref{hyp:subsonic}.
Suppose that $c_k\notin U([0,h_{\mathrm{a}}])$ and set $d:=\operatorname{dist}\bigl(c_k,U([0,h_{\mathrm{a}}])\bigr)>0$, the distance from $c_k$ to the range $U([0,h_{\mathrm{a}}])$ of $U$.
There are $r_\ast\in(0,\min\{d/4,c_k/2\})$ and $m_F,M_F,M_F',C_Z>0$, depending only on the profiles, $d$, $k$, and $\delta_0$, such that for all $c\in\mathbb{C}$ with $|c-c_k|\le r_\ast$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:F-bounds}
\begin{gathered}
\operatorname{Re} F\ge m_F>0,\qquad
\operatorname{Re} V^2\ge\tfrac12d^2,\qquad
|F|+|V^2/\bar{\rho}_0|\le M_F,\\
|\operatorname{Im} F|+k^2|\operatorname{Im}(V^2/\bar{\rho}_0)|\le M_F'\,|\operatorname{Im} c| .
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Moreover, the following hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item\label{it:coercive-nozero} The top-normalized solution satisfies $w(0;k,c)\neq0$.
In particular Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} holds on the real interval $[c_k-r_\ast,c_k+r_\ast]$, and $Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,\cdot)$ is holomorphic on the whole neighborhood $\{|c-c_k|<r_\ast\}$ of $c_k$ in $\mathbb{C}$.
\item\label{it:coercive-real} $Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)\in\mathbb{R}$ for real $c$ with $|c-c_k|\le r_\ast$.
\item\label{it:coercive-slope} $|\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)|\le C_Z\,|\operatorname{Im} c|$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
\emph{Bounds in Equation~\eqref{eq:F-bounds}.} For $|c-c_k|\le r_\ast\le d/4$ we have $|U-\operatorname{Re} c|\ge3d/4$ and $|\operatorname{Im} c|\le r_\ast$, so $\operatorname{Re} V^2=(U-\operatorname{Re} c)^2-(\operatorname{Im} c)^2\ge\tfrac9{16}d^2-\tfrac1{16}d^2 =\tfrac12d^2$, and with $F=a^2V^2/(\bar{\rho}_0(a^2-V^2))$,
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{Re}\bigl[V^2\,(a^2-V^2)^{\ast}\bigr]
=a^2\operatorname{Re} V^2-|V|^4
\ge a^2\bigl[(U-\operatorname{Re} c)^2-r_\ast^2\bigr]
-\bigl[(U-\operatorname{Re} c)^2+r_\ast^2\bigr]^2 ,
\end{equation}
and since $(U-\operatorname{Re} c)^2+r_\ast^2\le(1-\tfrac12\delta_0)a^2$ for $r_\ast$ small by Hypothesis \ref{hyp:subsonic}, the right-hand side is bounded below by $a^2\bigl[\tfrac12\delta_0(U-\operatorname{Re} c)^2-2r_\ast^2\bigr] \ge a^2\bigl[\tfrac{9}{32}\delta_0d^2-2r_\ast^2\bigr]>0$ for $r_\ast$ small.
Dividing by $\bar{\rho}_0|a^2-V^2|^2$ gives $\operatorname{Re} F\ge m_F>0$.
The upper bounds follow from compactness of $[0,h_{\mathrm{a}}]\times\{|c-c_k|\le r_\ast\}$ and the positive lower bounds for $\bar{\rho}_0$ and $|a^2-V^2|$.
The last bound in Equation~\eqref{eq:F-bounds} holds because $\operatorname{Im} V^2=-2(U-\operatorname{Re} c)\operatorname{Im} c$ and $\operatorname{Im} F=-2\operatorname{Im} c\,(U-\operatorname{Re} c)\,a^4/(\bar{\rho}_0|a^2-V^2|^2)$.
Note also $\operatorname{Re}(V^2/\bar{\rho}_0)=\operatorname{Re} V^2/\bar{\rho}_0\ge d^2/(2\sup\bar{\rho}_0)=:m_V>0$.
In particular $V$, $a^2-V^2$, and $F$ have no zeros on $[0,h_{\mathrm{a}}]\times\{|c-c_k|\le r_\ast\}$, so the coefficients of the system in Equation~\eqref{eq:wchi} are continuous in $z$ and holomorphic in $c$ on $\{|c-c_k|<r_\ast\}$, including at real phase speeds.
Hence the top-normalized solution and $Z_{\mathrm{a}}$ extend holomorphically there whenever $w(0;k,\cdot)\neq0$.

For \ref{it:coercive-nozero}, suppose $w(0;k,c)=0$.
Then $X=\bar{\rho}_0\,w/(\mathrm{i} kV)$ solves $(FX')'=k^2(V^2/\bar{\rho}_0)X$ with $X(0)=X(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$.
The reduction of \cref{prop:SLform}\ref{it:X} is valid for the present $c$, because its computation uses only $V\neq0$ and $a^2-V^2\neq0$, which hold for every $c$ with $|c-c_k|\le r_\ast$, including the real phase speeds, for which there is no critical level.
When we test the equation with $X^{\ast}$ over $(0,h_{\mathrm{a}})$ and integrate by parts, we obtain
\begin{equation}
\int_0^{h_{\mathrm{a}}}\Bigl[F|X'|^2+k^2\tfrac{V^2}{\bar{\rho}_0}|X|^2\Bigr]=0 ,
\end{equation}
whose real part is at least $m_F\|X'\|_{L^2}^2+k^2m_V\|X\|_{L^2}^2$ by Equation~\eqref{eq:F-bounds}, so $X\equiv0$, hence $(w,\bar{\chi})\equiv0$, contradicting the values at $z=h_{\mathrm{a}}$ given in Equation~\eqref{eq:canonical}.
For real $c_r$ in the interval $[c_k-r_\ast,c_k+r_\ast]$ there are no critical levels, so the limit solution of \cref{prop:limit-solutions} is the top-normalized solution at $c=c_r$ itself and Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} follows.

For \ref{it:coercive-real}, when $c$ is real the coefficients of the system in Equation~\eqref{eq:wchi} and the values at $z=h_{\mathrm{a}}$ given in Equation~\eqref{eq:canonical} are real, so $(w,\bar{\chi})$ is real-valued and the quotient in Equation~\eqref{eq:Za-formula} is real.

For \ref{it:coercive-slope}, normalize $\xi(0)=1$, which is possible by \ref{it:coercive-nozero}, so $X(0)=1$, $X(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$.
Write $X=\Upsilon+\tilde X$ with the lift $\Upsilon(z):=(h_{\mathrm{a}}-z)/h_{\mathrm{a}}$, a function with the same boundary values as $X$, and $\tilde X(0)=\tilde X(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$.
When we test the equation with $\tilde X^{\ast}$ and integrate by parts, we obtain
\begin{equation}
\int_0^{h_{\mathrm{a}}}\Bigl[F|\tilde X'|^2+k^2\tfrac{V^2}{\bar{\rho}_0}|\tilde X|^2\Bigr]
=-\int_0^{h_{\mathrm{a}}}\Bigl[F\Upsilon'(\tilde X')^{\ast}
+k^2\tfrac{V^2}{\bar{\rho}_0}\Upsilon\tilde X^{\ast}\Bigr].
\end{equation}
Taking real parts, using Equation~\eqref{eq:F-bounds}, and absorbing the right-hand side by Young's inequality gives $\|\tilde X\|_{H^1}\le C$, hence $\|X\|_{H^1}\le C$, with $C$ independent of $c$.
When we test with $X^{\ast}$ and use $X(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$ and $X(0)=1$, we obtain
\begin{equation}
\bar{q}(0)=F(0)X'(0)
=-\int_0^{h_{\mathrm{a}}}
\Bigl[F|X'|^2+k^2\tfrac{V^2}{\bar{\rho}_0}|X|^2\Bigr]\,\mathrm{d} z ,
\end{equation}
so by Equation~\eqref{eq:F-bounds}, $|\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)|=|\operatorname{Im}\bar{q}(0)|\le CM_F'\,|\operatorname{Im} c|=:C_Z|\operatorname{Im} c|$.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:necessity}
Assume Hypotheses \ref{hyp:reg}, \ref{hyp:noslip}, and \ref{hyp:subsonic}, and suppose that $c_k\notin U([0,h_{\mathrm{a}}])$.
With $r_\ast$ as in \cref{lem:coercive}, there is $\varepsilon_k>0$ such that the following hold for all $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_k)$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item\label{it:nec-neutral} Every normal mode of the linearized problem (\cref{prop:reduction}) with wavenumber $k$ and $|c-c_k|\le r_\ast$ has $\operatorname{Im} c=0$.
\item\label{it:nec-persist} The dispersion relation $D(k,\cdot,\varepsilon)=0$ has exactly one root $c_\varepsilon$ in the closed neighborhood $\{|c-c_k|\le r_\ast\}$ of $c_k$.
It is real and simple, and it is real-analytic in $\varepsilon$, that is, given by a convergent power series in $\varepsilon$, with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:neutral-branch}
c_\varepsilon=c_k+\varepsilon\,\frac{Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c_k)}{2kc_k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})}+O(\varepsilon^{2}),
\qquad
Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c_k)\in\mathbb{R}.
\end{equation}
Without a critical layer, the root near $c_k$ persists but remains real, so that the corresponding mode is neutral.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
For \ref{it:nec-neutral}, let $(w,c)$ be a normal mode with $|c-c_k|\le r_\ast$.
Suppose $\operatorname{Im} c\neq0$.
Then \cref{prop:reduction} gives $\hat\zeta\neq0$, and we normalize $\hat\zeta=1$.
When we take imaginary parts of the dispersion relation in Equation~\eqref{eq:dispersion} and use \cref{lem:coercive}\ref{it:coercive-slope}, we obtain
\begin{equation}
2k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\,\operatorname{Re} c\,|\operatorname{Im} c|
=|\operatorname{Im}D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c)|=\varepsilon\,|\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)|\le\varepsilon\,C_Z\,|\operatorname{Im} c| .
\end{equation}
Since $\operatorname{Re} c\ge c_k-r_\ast\ge c_k/2>0$, choosing $\varepsilon_k:=kc_k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})/C_Z$ forces $\operatorname{Im} c=0$.

For \ref{it:nec-persist}, Equations~\eqref{eq:Dw-def} and \eqref{eq:ck} give the exact quadratic form $D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c)=k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\,(c^2-c_k^2)$.
Hence, on the neighborhood $\{|c-c_k|\le r_\ast\}$, the function $D(k,c,\varepsilon)=k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\,(c^2-c_k^2)-\varepsilonZ_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)$ is holomorphic in $c$ by \cref{lem:coercive}\ref{it:coercive-nozero} and affine in $\varepsilon$, with $D(k,c_k,0)=0$ and $\partial_cD(k,c_k,0)=2kc_k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\neq0$.
The holomorphic implicit function theorem gives $\varepsilon_1>0$ and a unique analytic function $\varepsilon\mapsto c_\varepsilon$, defined for $|\varepsilon|<\varepsilon_1$, with $c_\varepsilon=c_k$ and $\partial_\varepsilon c_\varepsilon=Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c_k)/(2kc_k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}}))$ at $\varepsilon=0$, which gives Equation~\eqref{eq:neutral-branch}.
Uniqueness in the whole neighborhood, and simplicity, follow from Rouch\'e's theorem on the circle $|c-c_k|=r_\ast$.
On this circle, $|D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c)|=k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\,|c-c_k|\,|c+c_k|\ge k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\,r_\ast c_k$, since $|c+c_k|\ge2c_k-r_\ast\ge c_k$, while $\varepsilon|Z_{\mathrm{a}}|\le\varepsilon M_\ast$ with $M_\ast:=\max_{|c-c_k|\le r_\ast}|Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)|<\infty$.
For $\varepsilon<\varepsilon_k':=k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})r_\ast c_k/M_\ast$ the functions $D$ and $D_{\mathrm{w}}$ have equally many zeros in this neighborhood, namely the one simple zero $c_k$ of $D_{\mathrm{w}}$.
The other root $-c_k$ of $D_{\mathrm{w}}$ lies outside this neighborhood because $r_\ast\le c_k/2$.
The function $Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,\cdot)$ is real on the real interval $[c_k-r_\ast,c_k+r_\ast]$ (\cref{lem:coercive}\ref{it:coercive-real}) and holomorphic on this neighborhood, so the Schwarz reflection principle gives $Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c^{\ast})=Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)^{\ast}$ and $D(k,c^{\ast},\varepsilon)=D(k,c,\varepsilon)^{\ast}$.
The unique zero in this neighborhood is therefore real.
After shrinking $\varepsilon_k$ to $\min\{\varepsilon_k,\varepsilon_1,\varepsilon_k'\}$, all preceding estimates hold simultaneously.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:rouche}
Let $c_0\in\mathbb{R}$, $r>0$, and $D^{+}:=\{c\in\mathbb{C}:\,|c-c_0|<r,\ \operatorname{Im} c>0\}$, with closure $\overline{D^{+}}$ and boundary $\partial D^{+}$.
Let $\varphi,\psi$ be holomorphic in $D^{+}$ and continuous on $\overline{D^{+}}$, with $|\varphi-\psi|<|\psi|$ on $\partial D^{+}$.
Then $\varphi$ and $\psi$ have finitely many zeros in $D^{+}$ and equally many, counted with multiplicity, and neither vanishes on $\partial D^{+}$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Set $\mathfrak m:=\min_{\partial D^{+}}\bigl(|\psi|-|\varphi-\psi|\bigr)>0$.
Since $\varphi$ and $\psi$ are uniformly continuous on the compact set $\overline{D^{+}}$, they have a common modulus of continuity $\omega_0$, that is, a nondecreasing function on $[0,\infty)$ with $\omega_0(t)\to0$ as $t\downarrow0$ such that $|\varphi(c)-\varphi(c')|\le\omega_0(|c-c'|)$ and $|\psi(c)-\psi(c')|\le\omega_0(|c-c'|)$ for all $c,c'\in\overline{D^{+}}$.
For $t\in(0,r/2)$ let $D_t:=\{c:\,|c-c_0|<r-t,\ \operatorname{Im} c>t\}$, a convex set with $\overline{D_t}\subset D^{+}$ whose boundary is a piecewise-smooth simple closed curve.
Every $c\in\overline{D^{+}}\setminus D_t$ lies within distance $t$ of $\partial D^{+}$.
If $\operatorname{Im} c\le t$, its vertical projection to the real diameter lies in $\partial D^{+}$ at distance $\le t$.
If $|c-c_0|\ge r-t$, its radial projection to the semicircle does, because the projection preserves the sign of the imaginary part.
Hence, for such $c$ with nearest boundary point $c'$,
\begin{equation}
|\psi(c)|\ge|\psi(c')|-\omega_0(t)\ge\mathfrak m+|\varphi-\psi|(c')-\omega_0(t),
\qquad
|\varphi-\psi|(c)\le|\varphi-\psi|(c')+2\omega_0(t),
\end{equation}
so that $|\varphi-\psi|(c)<|\psi(c)|$ and $|\varphi(c)|\ge|\psi(c)|-|\varphi-\psi|(c)\ge\mathfrak m-3\omega_0(t)>0$ whenever $3\omega_0(t)<\mathfrak m$.
Choose such a $t$.
Then $\varphi$ and $\psi$ have no zeros in $\overline{D^{+}}\setminus D_t$, in particular none on $\partial D^{+}$, the strict inequality $|\varphi-\psi|<|\psi|$ holds on $\partial D_t$, and $\varphi,\psi$ are holomorphic on a neighborhood of $\overline{D_t}\subset D^{+}$.
Rouch\'e's theorem on $D_t$, whose boundary is a simple closed curve, gives equal, finite zero counts there, which are the zero counts in $D^{+}$.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:main}
Let $k>0$ and assume Hypotheses \ref{hyp:reg}--\ref{hyp:sigma}.
Suppose
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mainhyp}
\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)>0,
\end{equation}
with $Z_{\mathrm{a}}^+$ the boundary value of \cref{thm:limabs}.
Let $r_0$ and $M_Z$ be as in \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}, with $r_0$ shrunk once more so that $r_0\le c_k$ and $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_r)\ge\tfrac12\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)>0$ on the real interval $[c_k-r_0,c_k+r_0]$, and set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c1}
d_1:=\partial_cD_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c_k)=2kc_k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}}),
\qquad
c_1:=\frac{Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)}{d_1},
\qquad
R:=\max\Bigl\{\frac{4M_Z}{d_1},\ |c_1|+c_k\Bigr\}.
\end{equation}
Then there are $\varepsilon_0>0$ and $C_0>0$ such that the following hold for every $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0)$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item\label{it:main-exist} The dispersion relation $D(k,\cdot,\varepsilon)=D_{\mathrm{w}}-\varepsilonZ_{\mathrm{a}}$ has exactly one root $c_\varepsilon$ in the set $D^{+}_{r_0}:=\{|c-c_k|<r_0,\ \operatorname{Im} c>0\}$, whose closure is the set $\mathbb D^{+}_{r_0}$ of \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}.
It is simple.
Every root in $D^{+}_{r_0}$, hence $c_\varepsilon$ itself, satisfies $|c-c_k|\le(2M_Z/d_1)\,\varepsilon$.
There are no roots on the real interval $[c_k-r_0,c_k+r_0]$ in the sense of boundary values, that is, $D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c_r)\neq\varepsilonZ_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_r)$ there.
Equivalently, the holomorphic dispersion function $\mathfrak{E}(k,\cdot,\varepsilon)$ of \cref{def:determinant} has exactly one zero in $D^{+}_{r_0}$ (\cref{lem:determinant}\ref{it:det-nonvanish}).
\item\label{it:main-expansion} The root satisfies
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:expansion-rem}
  \bigl|c_\varepsilon-c_k-\varepsilon\,c_1\bigr|
  \le C_0\,\varepsilon^{2}\bigl(1+\log(1/\varepsilon)\bigr).
  \end{equation}
  In particular $\operatorname{Im} c_\varepsilon\ge\tfrac12\varepsilon\,\operatorname{Im} c_1>0$ for small $\varepsilon$, and the linearized problem has a normal mode growing like $e^{k\operatorname{Im} c_\varepsilon\,t}$.
\item\label{it:main-rate} The growth rate satisfies
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:growthrate}
  \gamma_\varepsilon:=k\,\operatorname{Im} c_\varepsilon
  =-\,\varepsilon\,\frac{\pi\,c_k\tanh(kh_{\mathrm{w}})}{2}
  \sum_{j=1}^{m}
  \frac{\bar{\rho}_0(z_j)\,Q(z_j)}{|U'(z_j)|}\,
  \bigl|\hat y(z_j)\bigr|^{2}
  \;+\;O\bigl(\varepsilon^{2}(1+\log(1/\varepsilon))\bigr),
  \end{equation}
  with $Q=U''+(g/a^2)U'=\bar{\rho}_0(U'/\bar{\rho}_0)'$ and $\hat y$ the interface-normalized limiting solution of \cref{thm:limabs}.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Let $c$ range over the closed set $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$ of \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}, the closure of $D^{+}_{r_0}$, on which $Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,\cdot)$ is holomorphic in the interior, continuous up to the boundary, and bounded by $M_Z$.

Set $\ell(c):=d_1\bigl(c-c_k-\varepsilon c_1\bigr)$, whose unique zero $c_k+\varepsilon c_1$ lies in the open upper half-plane ($\operatorname{Im} c_1=\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^+(k,c_k)/d_1>0$) and in $D^{+}_{R\varepsilon}$ ($|c_1|<R$).
By Equations~\eqref{eq:Dw-def} and \eqref{eq:ck}, $D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c)=k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\,(c^{2}-c_k^{2})$ exactly, so $D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c)-d_1(c-c_k)=k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})(c-c_k)^{2}$, and \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}\ref{it:Z-modulus} gives, for $|c-c_k|\le R\varepsilon$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:D-ell}
|D(k,c,\varepsilon)-\ell(c)|
\le k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\,R^2\varepsilon^2+\varepsilon\,\omega(R\varepsilon)
\le C_R\,\varepsilon^{2}\bigl(1+\log(1/\varepsilon)\bigr)
=:\varrho(\varepsilon),
\end{equation}
where the constant $C_R$ absorbs $\log(R/V_\ast)$.

On the semicircle $|c-c_k|=R\varepsilon$, $\operatorname{Im} c\ge0$, we have $|\ell(c)|\ge d_1(R-|c_1|)\varepsilon\ge d_1c_k\varepsilon$.
On the real interval $[c_k-R\varepsilon,c_k+R\varepsilon]$, we have $|\ell(c_r)|\ge d_1\varepsilon\,\operatorname{Im} c_1$.
Hence for $\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0$ small,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundary-margin}
\varrho(\varepsilon)<d_1\,\min\{c_k,\operatorname{Im} c_1\}\,\varepsilon\le|\ell|
\qquad\text{on the whole boundary of $D^{+}_{R\varepsilon}$.}
\end{equation}

If $D(k,c,\varepsilon)=0$ with $c\in D^{+}_{r_0}$, then $|D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c)|=\varepsilon|Z_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)|\le\varepsilon M_Z$.
Since $r_0\le c_k$ gives $|c+c_k|\ge2c_k-|c-c_k|\ge c_k$, we have $|D_{\mathrm{w}}(k,c)|=k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})|c-c_k||c+c_k|\ge\tfrac{d_1}2|c-c_k|$, whence $|c-c_k|\le(2M_Z/d_1)\,\varepsilon\le\tfrac12R\varepsilon$.
On the real interval $[c_k-r_0,c_k+r_0]$, a root in the sense of boundary values would require $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_r)=0$, since $D_{\mathrm{w}}$ is real there, and this is excluded by $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}\ge\tfrac12\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)>0$.

Apply \cref{lem:rouche} with $D^{+}=D^{+}_{R\varepsilon}$, $\varphi=D(k,\cdot,\varepsilon)$, and $\psi=\ell$.
By Equations~\eqref{eq:D-ell}--\eqref{eq:boundary-margin} its assumption holds, and $\ell$ has exactly one zero there, which is simple because $\ell$ is affine.
Hence $D$ has exactly one root $c_\varepsilon$ in $D^{+}_{R\varepsilon}$, and it is simple.
By the localization, every root in $D^{+}_{r_0}$ satisfies $|c-c_k|\le\tfrac12R\varepsilon$, hence lies in $D^{+}_{R\varepsilon}$, so $c_\varepsilon$ is the only root in $D^{+}_{r_0}$.
The statement about $\mathfrak{E}$ follows from \cref{lem:determinant}\ref{it:det-nonvanish}, since $w(0;k,\cdot)\neq0$ on $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$ (\cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}\ref{it:Z-nopole}).

Since $D(k,c_\varepsilon,\varepsilon)=0$ and $|c_\varepsilon-c_k|\le R\varepsilon$,
\begin{equation}
d_1\,|c_\varepsilon-c_k-\varepsilon c_1|=|\ell(c_\varepsilon)|
=|\ell(c_\varepsilon)-D(k,c_\varepsilon,\varepsilon)|\le\varrho(\varepsilon),
\end{equation}
which is Equation~\eqref{eq:expansion-rem} with $C_0:=C_R/d_1$.
Each root with $\operatorname{Im} c>0$ is a normal mode by \cref{lem:determinant}\ref{it:det-modes}.
Item \ref{it:main-rate} follows by inserting the residue formula in Equation~\eqref{eq:residue} at $c_r=c_k$ into the definition of $c_1$ in Equation~\eqref{eq:c1}.

The choice of $\varepsilon_0$ must satisfy three requirements.
(a) $R\varepsilon_0\le r_0/2$, so that $D^{+}_{R\varepsilon}$ and the point $c_\varepsilon$ used in \cref{cor:simple} lie in the region where every estimate of \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}, including the derivative bound \ref{it:Z-deriv}, is available.
(b) The boundary margin in Equation~\eqref{eq:boundary-margin}, $\varrho(\varepsilon_0)<d_1\min\{c_k,\operatorname{Im} c_1\}\varepsilon_0$, holds.
(c) $\varrho(\varepsilon_0)\le\tfrac12 d_1\varepsilon_0\,\operatorname{Im} c_1$, which turns Equation~\eqref{eq:expansion-rem} into $\operatorname{Im} c_\varepsilon\ge\tfrac12\varepsilon\,\operatorname{Im} c_1$.
Choose $\varepsilon_0\in(0,1)$ small enough that (a), (b), and (c) hold.
Since $\varrho(\varepsilon)/\varepsilon=C_R\varepsilon(1+\log(1/\varepsilon))$ is increasing on $(0,1)$, requirements (b) and (c) then also hold with $\varepsilon_0$ replaced by any $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0)$.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:simple}
Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:main} there is $\varepsilon_0'\in(0,\varepsilon_0)$ such that
\begin{equation}
\bigl|\partial_cD(k,c_\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\bigr|\ \ge\ kc_k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})
=\tfrac12\,d_1,
\qquad \varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0'] .
\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
The derivative is $\partial_cD(k,c,\varepsilon)=2k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\,c-\varepsilon\,\partial_cZ_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c)$.
At $c=c_\varepsilon$ we bound the two terms.
The first term satisfies $|2k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})c_\varepsilon-d_1|=2k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})|c_\varepsilon-c_k|\le 2k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})R\varepsilon\le\tfrac14 d_1$ for small $\varepsilon$.
For the second term, by \cref{thm:main}\ref{it:main-expansion} and requirement (a) in the choice of $\varepsilon_0$ there, $\operatorname{Im} c_\varepsilon\ge\tfrac12\varepsilon\operatorname{Im} c_1$ and $|c_\varepsilon-c_k|\le R\varepsilon\le r_0/2$, so \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}\ref{it:Z-deriv} gives
\begin{equation}
\varepsilon\,\bigl|\partial_cZ_{\mathrm{a}}(k,c_\varepsilon)\bigr|
\le C\varepsilon\Bigl(1+\bigl|\log\bigl(\tfrac12\varepsilon\operatorname{Im} c_1/V_\ast\bigr)
\bigr|\Bigr)
\le C'\varepsilon\bigl(1+\log(1/\varepsilon)\bigr)
\ \longrightarrow\ 0,
\end{equation}
so this term is at most $\tfrac14 d_1$ for $\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0'$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:spectral}
Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:main}, for every $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0)$ the closed, densely defined operator $\mathbb{L}_k$ of \cref{def:modal-operator} (\cref{app:operator}) has the eigenvalue
\begin{equation}
\lambda_\varepsilon:=-\mathrm{i} k\,c_\varepsilon,
\end{equation}
with $\operatorname{Re}\lambda_\varepsilon=k\,\operatorname{Im} c_\varepsilon\ge\tfrac12\,\varepsilon\,k\,\operatorname{Im} c_1>0$ and with one-dimensional eigenspace spanned by the normal mode of \cref{thm:main}\ref{it:main-expansion}.
The eigenvalue is an isolated, algebraically simple point of the spectrum of $\mathbb{L}_k$ for every $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0)$.
For $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0']$, the root $c_\varepsilon$ also satisfies the quantitative derivative bound of \cref{cor:simple}.
Thus the linearized problem is unstable at wavenumber $k$ in the sense of \cref{rem:spaces}, since $\mathbb{L}_k$ has an eigenvalue with positive real part.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
By \cref{thm:main}\ref{it:main-exist} and \cref{lem:determinant}\ref{it:det-nonvanish}, $\mathfrak{E}(k,c_\varepsilon,\varepsilon)=0$ with $\operatorname{Im} c_\varepsilon>0$.
\cref{thm:pointspec} converts this into the eigenvalue statement, including the dimension of the eigenspace.
For algebraic simplicity, \cref{thm:main}\ref{it:main-exist} counts one root with multiplicity, so $c_\varepsilon$ is a simple zero of $D(k,\cdot,\varepsilon)$ for every $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0)$, that is, $\partial_cD(k,c_\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\neq0$.
Since $w(0;k,c_\varepsilon)\neq0$ (\cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}\ref{it:Z-nopole}), the factorization in Equation~\eqref{eq:Edet-factor} gives $\partial_c\mathfrak{E}(k,c_\varepsilon,\varepsilon) =w(0;k,c_\varepsilon)\,\partial_cD(k,c_\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\neq0$, because the derivative of $w(0;k,\cdot)$ multiplies $D(k,c_\varepsilon,\varepsilon)=0$.
Hence \cref{cor:algsimple} applies.
The quantitative bound for $\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0'$ is \cref{cor:simple}.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:sufficient}
Assume Hypotheses \ref{hyp:reg}--\ref{hyp:subsonic} and \ref{hyp:sigma}, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sign-hyp}
c_k\,Q(z_j)\le0\quad\text{for all }j,
\qquad
c_k\,Q(z_m)<0\quad\text{at the highest critical level.}
\end{equation}
Then Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} and the condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:mainhyp} hold automatically, and all conclusions of \cref{thm:main} follow.
If instead the strict inequality in Equation~\eqref{eq:sign-hyp} is available only at some level $j_0<m$, the same conclusions follow under the additional assumption $w_\ast(z_{j_0})\neq0$, a nondegeneracy that is checkable numerically for given profiles.
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
By \cref{lem:positivity}, under the sign condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:sign-hyp} the limit solution has $u_1(z_m)>0$ and $u_1>0$ on $[0,z_m)$, where $u_1=|w_\ast|^2$ is the quantity $u_1$ of \cref{prop:local}\ref{it:quadratic} for the limit solution.
In particular $u_1(0)>0$, which is Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres}.
Since $c_k>0$, the sign condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:sign-hyp} makes every term of the sum $\sum_j\bar{\rho}_0 Q|\hat y|^2/|U'|$ in Equation~\eqref{eq:residue} nonpositive, and the term $j=m$ strictly negative because $u_1(z_m)>0$.
Hence $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^+(k,c_k)=-\pi c_k^2\sum_j\bar{\rho}_0 Q|\hat y|^2/|U'|>0$, which is the condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:mainhyp}.
Under the alternative assumption $w_\ast(z_{j_0})\neq0$, the flux accumulation in the proof of \cref{lem:positivity} applies with the first nonzero increment at $z_{j_0}$ instead of $z_m$.
Because all jumps have the same sign, they give $\mathcal{W}\neq0$ on $[0,z_{j_0})$, hence $u_1>0$ there, including Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} at $z=0$.
The $j_0$-term of the sum in Equation~\eqref{eq:residue} is then strictly negative, which gives the condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:mainhyp} again.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:monotone}
Let $U\in C^4([0,h_{\mathrm{a}}])$ with $U(0)=0$ and $U'>0$ on $[0,h_{\mathrm{a}}]$.
Let the neutral atmosphere satisfy Hypothesis \ref{hyp:reg}, and let the surface tension satisfy Hypothesis \ref{hyp:sigma}.
Let $k>0$ be such that $c_k\in U((0,h_{\mathrm{a}}))$ and Hypothesis \ref{hyp:subsonic} holds.
Since $U'>0$, there is exactly one critical level $z_c$, and it is regular.
Assume
\begin{equation}\label{eq:criterion}
Q(z_c)
=U''(z_c)+\frac{g}{a^2(z_c)}\,U'(z_c)
=\bar{\rho}_0(z_c)\Bigl(\frac{U'}{\bar{\rho}_0}\Bigr)'(z_c)
\;<\;0 .
\end{equation}
Then Hypotheses \ref{hyp:critical}--\ref{hyp:nonres} and the condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:mainhyp} hold automatically, and for $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0)$ the dispersion relation has exactly one root in the set $D^{+}_{r_0}$ of \cref{thm:main}, and this root is simple, with
\begin{gather*}
\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)
=-\pi c_k^{2}\,\frac{\bar{\rho}_0(z_c)\,Q(z_c)}{|U'(z_c)|}\,
\bigl|\hat y(z_c)\bigr|^{2}>0,
\qquad
c_\varepsilon=c_k+\varepsilon\,c_1+O\bigl(\varepsilon^{2}(1+\log(1/\varepsilon))\bigr),\\
\gamma_\varepsilon=k\,\operatorname{Im} c_\varepsilon
=-\,\varepsilon\,\frac{\pi\,c_k\tanh(kh_{\mathrm{w}})}{2}\,
\frac{\bar{\rho}_0(z_c)\,Q(z_c)}{|U'(z_c)|}\,\bigl|\hat y(z_c)\bigr|^{2}
+O\bigl(\varepsilon^{2}(1+\log(1/\varepsilon))\bigr)>0,
\end{gather*}
and $\lambda_\varepsilon=-\mathrm{i} kc_\varepsilon$ is an isolated, algebraically simple eigenvalue of the closed operator $\mathbb{L}_k$ with $\operatorname{Re}\lambda_\varepsilon>0$ (\cref{cor:spectral}).
As $a\to\infty$, the criterion in Equation~\eqref{eq:criterion} reduces to Miles's condition $U''(z_c)<0$.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
The condition $U'>0$ makes every solution of $U=c_k$ regular and unique, which is Hypothesis \ref{hyp:critical} with $m=1$.
With $c_k>0$, the sign condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:sign-hyp} reduces to $Q(z_c)<0$, so \cref{cor:sufficient} supplies Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres}, the condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:mainhyp}, and the displayed value of $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)$.
The remaining statements are \cref{thm:main}, \cref{cor:simple}, and \cref{cor:spectral}.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{rem:mirror}
Here $c_k>0$ denotes the right-going root of $D_{\mathrm{w}}$ given by Equation~\eqref{eq:ck}.
The left-going root $-c_k$ is covered by the reflection $(x,U)\mapsto(-x,-U)$.
The same argument then applies to the reflected problem, whose critical levels are the solutions of $U=-c_k$, with the sign condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:sign-hyp} read with $c_k$ replaced by $-c_k$.
The two roots have independent sets of critical levels.
For the non-negative winds of \S\ref{sec:examples} the left-going wave has no critical level, and \cref{thm:necessity}, applied after reflection, keeps it neutral.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
\label{rem:reading}
Since $g/a^2>0$, for a wind profile that increases with height with shear decreasing upward ($U'>0>U''$) compressibility shifts the local coefficient $Q=U''+(g/a^2)U'$ upward.
While $Q(z_c)<0$ this reduces $|Q(z_c)|$ below $|U''(z_c)|$, and the criterion in Equation~\eqref{eq:criterion} fails altogether once
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cutoff-height}
\frac{g}{a^2(z_c)}\ \ge\ \frac{|U''(z_c)|}{U'(z_c)} .
\end{equation}
For a logarithmic profile $U=(u_\ast/\kappa_{\mathrm K})\log(1+z/z_0)$, where $u_\ast$ is the friction velocity, $\kappa_{\mathrm K}$ the von K\'arm\'an constant, and $z_0$ the roughness length, the condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:cutoff-height} holds beyond the height at which $z_c+z_0=a^2/g$.
For the bounded shear-layer profile $U=U_\infty(1-e^{-z/L})$ of limiting speed $U_\infty$ and shear depth $L$, it holds uniformly in $z$ when $a^2<gL$.
In the second case, consider a wavenumber that satisfies the assumptions of \cref{prop:suppression} and lies in the incompressible Miles band, which is the set of wavenumbers $k$ for which $c_k$ lies in the range of $U$ with $U''<0$ at the critical levels.
At such a wavenumber the sum in Equation~\eqref{eq:growthrate} is positive, so the leading Miles coefficient $\operatorname{Im} c_1$ changes sign and the local $O(\varepsilon)$ Miles bifurcation from $c_k$ is absent (\cref{prop:suppression}).
At the same time the growth rate is not a function of $Q(z_c)$ alone.
The amplitude $|\hat y(z_c)|^2$ and the weight $\bar{\rho}_0(z_c)$ are themselves modified by compressibility, and the coefficient $\bar{A}/\bar{\rho}_0=a^{2}/(a^{2}-V^{2})$ also changes the vertical boundary-value problem.
Hence the sign of the net correction is not decided by the local criterion alone.
For the parameters of wind waves over the sea, the compressible corrections are at most about $10^{-3}$ locally and $10^{-2}$ globally (\S\ref{subsec:polytropic-wind}, \cref{rem:two-effects}), and the condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:cutoff-height} would require critical layers $8.4\,\mathrm{km}$ above the sea surface.
Miles's incompressible criterion is therefore insensitive to compressibility for water waves, and \cref{thm:lowmach} makes this precise at each wavenumber.
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
As in \cite[Rem.~4.3]{BSWZ2016}, \cref{cor:sufficient} applies for every $k$ such that $c_k$ lies in the range of $U$ with $Q<0$ at the critical levels.
With surface tension the set of such $k$ is a bounded band, because large-$k$ waves are too fast, with $c_k\to\infty$.
Its edges are set by $c_k\in U([0,h_{\mathrm{a}}])$ as in the incompressible theory, except near the compressible cutoff in Equation~\eqref{eq:cutoff-height}, where the band is further truncated.
The Kelvin--Helmholtz mechanism is absent by Hypothesis \ref{hyp:noslip}.
The compressible Kelvin--Helmholtz problem for uniform wind is treated in closed form in \S\ref{subsec:KH} for comparison.
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:lowmach}
Let $K$ be a compact set of regular values of $U$ containing $c_k$, on which Hypothesis \ref{hyp:critical} holds and the incompressible problem satisfies Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres}.
Then there are $M_0,C>0$ such that for all $M\in(0,M_0]$ and $c_r\in K$ the compressible problem satisfies Hypotheses \ref{hyp:subsonic}--\ref{hyp:nonres} as well, its boundary value exists, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lowmach-rate}
\bigl|Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+,M}(k,c_r)-Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+,\mathrm{inc}}(k,c_r)\bigr|
\;\le\;C\,M^{2} .
\end{equation}
Moreover, with $\mathbb D^{+}_{r_0}$ the closed set of \cref{cor:uniformM}, a closed neighborhood of $c_k$ in the closed upper half-plane of the form used in \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}, the estimate holds uniformly on that set,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lowmach-complex}
\sup_{c\in\mathbb D^{+}_{r_0}}
\bigl|Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{M}(k,c)-Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{\mathrm{inc}}(k,c)\bigr|
\;\le\;C\,M^{2} .
\end{equation}
Consequently $c_1(M)=c_1(0)+O(M^2)$, where $c_1(0)$ is the coefficient of the incompressible theory.
If moreover the incompressible problem is unstable at $k$ in the sense of \cref{thm:main}, that is $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+,\mathrm{inc}}(k,c_k)>0$, then there is $M_1\in(0,M_0]$ such that for all $M\le M_1$ the compressible problem satisfies the condition in Equation~\eqref{eq:mainhyp} as well, with $\operatorname{Im} c_1(M)\ge\tfrac12\,\operatorname{Im} c_1(0)>0$.
Both problems then possess the unstable root of \cref{thm:main}.
The expansion in Equation~\eqref{eq:expansion-rem} holds with constants $(\varepsilon_0,C_0)$ independent of $M\le M_1$,
\begin{equation}
c_\varepsilon(M)=c_k+\varepsilon\,c_1(M)+O\bigl(\varepsilon^{2}(1+\log(1/\varepsilon))\bigr)
\qquad\text{uniformly in }M\le M_1.
\end{equation}
For each $\varepsilon$, the roots converge at the rate
\begin{equation}\label{eq:root-rate}
\bigl|c_\varepsilon(M)-c_\varepsilon(0)\bigr|\;\le\;C\,\varepsilon\,M^{2},
\qquad 0<\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0,\ 0<M\le M_1 .
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:uniformconst}
For $M_0$ small enough, the atmospheres in Equation~\eqref{eq:M-family} satisfy the subsonic gap of Hypothesis \ref{hyp:subsonic} uniformly in $M\in(0,M_0]$.
The coefficient functions of the local representation in Equation~\eqref{eq:rep}, namely $b_M$, $E_M$, $f_M=1/(\bar{A}_ME_M)$, $g_{1,M}$, $g_{2,M}$, together with the coefficients of the system in Equation~\eqref{eq:wchi-recall} on intervals containing no critical level, are bounded in $C^2$, except $g_{1,M}$, which is bounded in $C^1$, uniformly for $M\in[0,M_0]$, with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:coef-lip}
\|b_M\|_{C^2}+\|f_M-1\|_{C^2}+\|g_{1,M}-g_1^{\mathrm{inc}}\|_{C^1}
+\|g_{2,M}-g_2^{\mathrm{inc}}\|_{C^1}\le C M^2 ,
\end{equation}
where the coefficients at $M=0$ are those of the Rayleigh problem in Equation~\eqref{eq:rayleigh-limit}, which we mark with the superscript $\mathrm{inc}$, namely $f^{\mathrm{inc}}=1$, $g_1^{\mathrm{inc}}=(1/U')'$, and $g_2^{\mathrm{inc}}=k^2/U'$.
Consequently the constants of \cref{lem:fixedpoint}, \cref{prop:local}, \cref{prop:convergence}, \cref{prop:limit-solutions}, and \cref{thm:limabs}, including $\varsigma_0$ and the contraction constant $\tfrac12$, can be chosen uniformly in $M\in[0,M_0]$, and so can $\varsigma$ and $\delta_1$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
The gap holds uniformly once $M_0$ is small, because $\operatorname{Re}(a_M^2-V^2)\ge a_M^2-|U-c|^2$, $a_M^2\ge a_\ast^2\hat a_-/M^2$, and $|U-c|^2\le(2a_\ast+r_0)^2$ for every complex $c$ with $|c-c_k|\le r_0$.
The bounds in Equation~\eqref{eq:coef-lip} follow from $b_M=gM^2/(a_\ast^2\hat a)$, from $\bar{A}_M=\bar\rho_{0,M}\bigl(1-M^2V^2/(a_\ast^2\hat a)\bigr)^{-1}$, and from $\bar\rho_{0,M}$ and $E_M$, each of which is $1+O(M^2)$ in $C^2$ by Equation~\eqref{eq:M-family} and $\hat a\ge\hat a_-$, together with the definitions in Equation~\eqref{eq:g-def}, which are linear in $f$.
Every constant in the cited results depends only on these norms, the gap, $|U'(z_j)|^{-1}$, and $k$.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
By \cref{lem:uniformconst}, the critical-layer theory of \S\ref{sec:criticallayer} applies to every atmosphere in Equation~\eqref{eq:M-family} with constants uniform in $M\in[0,M_0]$.

On intervals containing no critical level, the coefficients of the system in Equation~\eqref{eq:wchi-recall} for the atmospheres in Equation~\eqref{eq:M-family} differ by $O(M^2)$ in $C^0$.
The values at $z=h_{\mathrm{a}}$ given in Equation~\eqref{eq:canonical} also differ by $O(M^2)$, because $\bar{A}_M(h_{\mathrm{a}})=1+O(M^2)$, while $\bar{A}=1$ in the limiting problem in Equation~\eqref{eq:rayleigh-limit}.
Hence the solution maps on these intervals differ in norm by $O(M^2)$ by Gr\"onwall's inequality.

Across a critical layer we apply \cref{lem:coef-perturb} of \cref{app:proofs}, including its uniform bounds on the endpoint evaluation matrices and their inverses, valid for every $\delta\in[0,\delta_1]$, to the coefficient tuples $\mathfrak a_M:=(b_M,\bar\rho_{0,M},f_M,g_{1,M},g_{2,M})$ and $\mathfrak a^{\mathrm{inc}}:=(0,1,1,g_1^{\mathrm{inc}},g_2^{\mathrm{inc}})$ of the limiting problem.
Both tuples satisfy the first-order-system identities of \cref{lem:coef-perturb}, because both are generated by neutral atmospheres.
These identities state that $g_1$ and $g_2$ are obtained from $b$, $\bar{\rho}_0$, and $f$ by Equation~\eqref{eq:g-def}.
The two tuples also share $U$, $c$, the base point $z_j$, and the branch of the logarithm in Equation~\eqref{eq:branch}.
Their distance $\|\mathfrak a_M-\mathfrak a^{\mathrm{inc}}\|_{\mathcal X}$ is the sum of the $C^0(I_j)$ norms of the differences of $b$, $\bar{\rho}_0$, and $g_2$, the $C^1(I_j)$ norm of the difference of $f$, and the $C^{0,1}(I_j)$ norm of the difference of $g_1$.
It satisfies $\|\mathfrak a_M-\mathfrak a^{\mathrm{inc}}\|_{\mathcal X}\le CM^2$ by Equation~\eqref{eq:coef-lip} together with $\|\bar\rho_{0,M}-1\|_{C^1}\le CM^2$.
Hence the maps $T_\delta$ across the critical neighborhoods, their inverses, and the critical values $w_\ast(z_j)$ are $O(M^2)$-close to their incompressible counterparts, uniformly in $\delta\in[0,\delta_1]$ and on $K$.

When we compose the solution operators on intervals containing no critical level with the maps $T_\delta$ across the critical neighborhoods, from $z=h_{\mathrm{a}}$ down to $z=0$, the values $\bigl(w_\ast(0),\bar{\chi}_\ast(0),w_\ast(z_j)\bigr)$ of the normalized limit solution differ between $M$ and $0$ by at most $C_1M^2$, uniformly on $K$.
Since $m_0:=\min_K|w_\ast^{\mathrm{inc}}(0;\cdot)|>0$ by Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} for the incompressible problem and continuity on the compact set $K$, we shrink $M_0$ so that $C_1M_0^2\le m_0/2$.
Then $|w_\ast^{M}(0;c_r)|\ge m_0/2$ for all $M\in(0,M_0]$ and $c_r\in K$, which is Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} for the compressible problem.
With this common lower bound on the denominator, the quotient in Equation~\eqref{eq:Za-formula} gives Equation~\eqref{eq:lowmach-rate}.
For the complex version in Equation~\eqref{eq:lowmach-complex}, we also shrink $M_0$ so that the conclusions of \cref{cor:uniformM} hold for every $M\in(0,M_0]$.
\cref{cor:uniformM}\ref{it:uM-trace} gives a common positive lower bound for the interface traces $|w^{M}(0;k,c)|$ on $\mathbb D^{+}_{r_0}$.
Equation~\eqref{eq:lowmach-complex} is then \cref{cor:uniformM}\ref{it:uM-complex}, because the same composition applies at every $c=c_r+\mathrm{i}\delta$ with $\delta\in[0,\delta_1]$, because \cref{lem:coef-perturb} is uniform in $\delta$, and every point of $\mathbb D^{+}_{r_0}$ has $\operatorname{Im} c\le r_0\le\delta_1$.

The leading coefficient satisfies $c_1(M)-c_1(0) =\bigl(Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+,M}-Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+,\mathrm{inc}}\bigr)(k,c_k)/(2kc_k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}}))=O(M^2)$.
Assume $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+,\mathrm{inc}}(k,c_k)>0$.
By Equation~\eqref{eq:lowmach-rate} there is $M_1\in(0,M_0]$ with $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+,M}(k,c_k)\ge\tfrac12\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+,\mathrm{inc}}(k,c_k)>0$, hence $\operatorname{Im} c_1(M)\ge\tfrac12\operatorname{Im} c_1(0)>0$, for all $M\le M_1$, so \cref{thm:main} applies to every $M\le M_1$.
By \cref{cor:uniformM} of \cref{app:proofs}, the atmospheres in Equation~\eqref{eq:M-family} admit a common closed neighborhood $\mathbb D^{+}_{r_0}$ of $c_k$ in the closed upper half-plane on which no $Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{M}(k,\cdot)$ has a pole, a common bound $M_Z$, and a common modulus of continuity $\omega(\cdot)$ at $c_k$.
Together with the uniform lower bound on $\operatorname{Im} c_1(M)$, every constant in the proof of \cref{thm:main}, including the constants $(\varepsilon_0,R,C_0)$ in Equations~\eqref{eq:c1} and \eqref{eq:expansion-rem}, can be chosen uniformly in $M\le M_1$, and equally at $M=0$.
The uniform choice of $(\varepsilon_0,R,C_0)$ also gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Im-floor}
\operatorname{Im} c_\varepsilon(M)\ \ge\ \tfrac12\,\varepsilon\,\operatorname{Im} c_1(M)
\ \ge\ \tfrac14\,\varepsilon\,\operatorname{Im} c_1(0),
\qquad
|c_\varepsilon(M)-c_k|\le R\varepsilon,
\qquad \varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0,\ M\le M_1 .
\end{equation}

For the estimate at a given $\varepsilon$, write $D_M:=D_{\mathrm{w}}-\varepsilonZ_{\mathrm{a}}^{M}$ and $D^{\mathrm{inc}}:=D_{\mathrm{w}}-\varepsilonZ_{\mathrm{a}}^{\mathrm{inc}}$.
Since $D_M(k,c_\varepsilon(M),\varepsilon)=0$,
\begin{equation}
\bigl|D^{\mathrm{inc}}(k,c_\varepsilon(M),\varepsilon)\bigr|
=\bigl|D^{\mathrm{inc}}-D_M\bigr|(k,c_\varepsilon(M),\varepsilon)
=\varepsilon\,\bigl|Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{M}-Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{\mathrm{inc}}\bigr|(k,c_\varepsilon(M))
\;\le\;C\varepsilon M^{2}
\end{equation}
by Equation~\eqref{eq:lowmach-complex}.
The straight segment from $c_\varepsilon(0)$ to $c_\varepsilon(M)$ stays in $\{|c-c_k|\le R\varepsilon,\ \operatorname{Im} c\ge\tfrac14\varepsilon\operatorname{Im} c_1(0)\}$, because both endpoints satisfy both constraints by Equation~\eqref{eq:Im-floor} and both constraints are preserved under convex combination.
On this segment,
\begin{equation}
\bigl|\partial_cD^{\mathrm{inc}}(k,c,\varepsilon)-d_1\bigr|
\le 2k\coth(kh_{\mathrm{w}})\,R\varepsilon
+\varepsilon\,C\Bigl(1+\bigl|\log\bigl(\tfrac14\varepsilon\operatorname{Im}
c_1(0)/V_\ast\bigr)\bigr|\Bigr)
\;\le\;\tfrac12\,d_1
\end{equation}
for $\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0$, after we shrink $\varepsilon_0$ once, independently of $M$, so that in addition $R\varepsilon_0\le r_0/2$.
The segment then lies in the region where \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}\ref{it:Z-deriv} is available for the incompressible problem, to which the critical-layer theory of \S\ref{sec:criticallayer} applies with the same constants.
Hence
\begin{align*}
&\bigl|D^{\mathrm{inc}}(k,c_\varepsilon(M),\varepsilon)-D^{\mathrm{inc}}(k,c_\varepsilon(0),\varepsilon)\bigr|\\
&\qquad=\Bigl|\int_0^1\partial_cD^{\mathrm{inc}}\bigl(k,(1-t)c_\varepsilon(0)+tc_\varepsilon(M),\varepsilon\bigr)\,\mathrm{d} t\Bigr|
\,\bigl|c_\varepsilon(M)-c_\varepsilon(0)\bigr|\\
&\qquad\ge\ \tfrac12\,d_1\,\bigl|c_\varepsilon(M)-c_\varepsilon(0)\bigr|,
\end{align*}
since the integral lies within $\tfrac12 d_1$ of $d_1$.
With $D^{\mathrm{inc}}(k,c_\varepsilon(0),\varepsilon)=0$ this gives $|c_\varepsilon(M)-c_\varepsilon(0)|\le(2C/d_1)\,\varepsilon M^{2}$, which is Equation~\eqref{eq:root-rate}.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
\label{rem:lowmach-scope}
The estimate in Equation~\eqref{eq:root-rate} controls the unstable root itself for $0<\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0$, pointwise in $k$.
The constants may depend on $k$, and no uniformity over a wavenumber interval is claimed.
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- remark-14: body_md -->
\label{rem:two-effects}
The scaling in Equation~\eqref{eq:M-family} sends the acoustic ratio $|V|^2/a^2$ and the hydrostatic ratio $gz_c/a^2$ to zero at order $M^2$.
The former enters through $\bar{A}=\bar{\rho}_0 a^2/(a^2-V^2)$, while the latter enters through $\bar{\rho}_0$ and $Q-U''=(g/a^2)U'$.
For ocean--atmosphere parameters, the global Mach parameter satisfies $M^2\lesssim10^{-2}$ for the wavenumbers of wind waves.
Here $\lesssim$ means that the left side is at most of the order of the right side.
The squared local wave Mach number $(c_k/a(z_c))^2$ and the hydrostatic ratio $gz_c/a(z_c)^2$ are each typically at most $10^{-3}$.
<!-- end remark-14 -->
<!-- proposition-10: body_md -->
\label{prop:KH}
Let the atmosphere have constant sound speed $a$ and density $\bar{\rho}_0=e^{-bz}$ with $b=g/a^2$ (\cref{ex:constant-sound-speed}), and let the wind be uniform, $U\equiv U_0$ for a constant $U_0$.
Here $V=U-c$ is the constant $U_0-c$, and we assume $V\neq0$ and the subsonicity condition $|V|^2<a^2$.
Then the mass-weighted displacement $X=\bar{\rho}_0\,\xi$ satisfies the constant-coefficient equation
\begin{equation}\label{eq:KH-ode}
X''+b\,X'-k^2\Bigl(1-\frac{V^2}{a^2}\Bigr)X=0 ,
\end{equation}
and with the impermeable top boundary at $h_{\mathrm{a}}$ the air-side pressure response is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:KH-Z}
Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{\mathrm{KH}}(k,c)
=\frac{a^2V^2}{a^2-V^2}\,
\frac{\mu_-+\mu_+\,\Theta}{1+\Theta}-g,
\qquad
\Theta:=-e^{(\mu_--\mu_+)h_{\mathrm{a}}},
\qquad
\mu_\pm:=-\frac b2\pm\nu,
\end{equation}
where $\nu:=\sqrt{b^2/4+k^2(1-V^2/a^2)}$ is the principal square root, $\sqrt{\zeta}=\exp(\tfrac12\operatorname{Log}\zeta)$ with $\operatorname{Log}$ from Equation~\eqref{eq:Log-def}.
It satisfies $\operatorname{Re}\nu>0$ and is well defined for $c$ in the complex subsonic neighborhood of Hypothesis \ref{hyp:subsonic}, on which $\operatorname{Re}(1-V^2/a^2)>0$.
As $h_{\mathrm{a}}\to\infty$, $Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{\mathrm{KH}}\to a^2V^2\mu_-/(a^2-V^2)-g$.
As $a\to\infty$, $Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{\mathrm{KH}}\to-kV^2\coth(kh_{\mathrm{a}})-g$, the finite-air-depth incompressible uniform-wind response.
Taking in addition $h_{\mathrm{a}}\to\infty$ gives $-kV^2-g$, the air-side term in the Kelvin--Helmholtz dispersion relation of \cite[Ex.~3.1]{BSWZ2016}.
<!-- end proposition-10 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
The derivation of Equation~\eqref{eq:Xform} in \cref{prop:SLform} and the definition of $Z_{\mathrm{a}}$ in Equation~\eqref{eq:Za-def} use only $V\neq0$ and $a^2-V^2\neq0$, not Hypothesis \ref{hyp:noslip}, and both conditions hold here for real and complex $c$ because $V$ is a nonzero constant and $|V|^2<a^2$.
Equation~\eqref{eq:KH-ode} is Equation~\eqref{eq:Xform} evaluated for this base state and divided by $F$, which is nonzero because $V\neq0$.
The boundary condition $w(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$ is $X(h_{\mathrm{a}})=0$, and Equation~\eqref{eq:KH-Z} follows from $\bar{q}=\bar{\rho}_0 FX'$ and Equation~\eqref{eq:Za-def}.
The limits follow by direct computation.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- proposition-11: body_md -->
\label{prop:suppression}
Let the atmosphere have constant sound speed $a$ (\cref{ex:constant-sound-speed}) and wind $U=U_\infty(1-e^{-z/L})$, $U_\infty>0$, and let $k$ be such that $c_k\in U((0,h_{\mathrm{a}}))$ with a single critical level and Hypothesis \ref{hyp:subsonic} holds.
If
\begin{equation}\label{eq:suppression-cond}
a^2<gL ,
\end{equation}
then $Q>0$ on all of $[0,h_{\mathrm{a}}]$ although $U''<0$ everywhere.
The limit solution satisfies $w_\ast(z_c)\neq0$ and Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} by \cref{lem:positivity} with the stated sign condition, and
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)
=-\pi c_k^2\,\frac{\bar{\rho}_0(z_c)Q(z_c)}{|U'(z_c)|}\,|\hat y(z_c)|^2\;<\;0 .
\end{equation}
Consequently $\operatorname{Im} c_1<0$ for the coefficient $c_1$ in Equation~\eqref{eq:c1}, and there are $\varepsilon_0,r_0>0$ such that for $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0)$ the dispersion relation has no root in the set $\{|c-c_k|\le r_0,\ \operatorname{Im} c>0\}$, and none on the real interval $[c_k-r_0,c_k+r_0]$ in the sense of boundary values.
At this admissible wavenumber $k$, the incompressible criterion $U''(z_c)<0$ of \cite{Miles1957,BSWZ2016} predicts growth, whereas the compressible dispersion relation has no $O(\varepsilon)$ unstable root bifurcating from $c_k$.
<!-- end proposition-11 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
Equation~\eqref{eq:Q-exp} with $a^2<gL$ gives $Q>0$.
Since all critical-level values $c_kQ(z_j)$ have the same sign, \cref{lem:positivity} applies and yields $w_\ast(z_c)\neq0$.
The same argument holds for every regular value $c_r$ near $c_k$, because $Q>0$ on $[0,h_{\mathrm{a}}]$.
Hence Hypothesis \ref{hyp:nonres} holds on a real interval about $c_k$, and \cref{thm:limabs} gives the displayed formula.
Choose $r_0$ and $M_Z$ from \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}, then shrink $r_0$ so that $r_0\le c_k$ and $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_r)\le\tfrac12\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)<0$ on the real interval $[c_k-r_0,c_k+r_0]$.
Since $D_{\mathrm{w}}$ is real there, there is no root in the sense of boundary values on this interval.
The localization estimate in the proof of \cref{thm:main} uses only $|Z_{\mathrm{a}}|\le M_Z$ and $r_0\le c_k$, which hold on the closed set $\mathbb D^+_{r_0}$ of \cref{prop:Zhalfdisc}.
It therefore places every root in $\{|c-c_k|\le r_0,\ \operatorname{Im} c>0\}$ inside $\{|c-c_k|\le\tfrac12R\varepsilon\}$, where $R=\max\{4M_Z/d_1,|c_1|+c_k\}$ is given by Equation~\eqref{eq:c1} and $R\varepsilon_0\le r_0$.
On the set $D^{+}_{R\varepsilon}$ defined after \cref{thm:main}, compare $D$ with the affine function $\ell(c)=d_1(c-c_k-\varepsilon c_1)$.
Because $\operatorname{Im} c_1<0$, the zero of $\ell$ lies in the lower half-plane.
The boundary estimate $|D-\ell|\le\varrho(\varepsilon)<d_1\min\{c_k,|\operatorname{Im} c_1|\}\varepsilon\le|\ell|$ uses only $\operatorname{Im} c_1\neq0$, so \cref{lem:rouche} gives no zero of $D$ in $D^{+}_{R\varepsilon}$.
Localization then excludes roots from the larger set $\{|c-c_k|\le r_0,\ \operatorname{Im} c>0\}$.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- remark-15: body_md -->
\label{rem:suppression}
\cref{prop:suppression} excludes an unstable root near $c_k$ but does not produce a decaying eigenvalue.
Because the coefficients are real, complex phase speeds with $\operatorname{Im} c\neq0$ occur in conjugate pairs (\cref{lem:determinant}\ref{it:det-hol}).
An isolated lower-half-plane partner would require the excluded upper-half-plane root.
The negative value $\operatorname{Im}Z_{\mathrm{a}}^{+}(k,c_k)$ is the signed pressure work on a prescribed neutral wave, not an eigenvalue decay rate.
<!-- end remark-15 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:coef-perturb}
In this setting, the following bounds hold uniformly in $\delta\in[0,\delta_1]$ and in $c_r$ on compact sets of regular values.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item\label{it:cp-fixed} The fixed points of \cref{lem:fixedpoint} with the same constants $(\alpha,\beta)$ satisfy
\begin{equation}
\|w_{\mathfrak a}-w_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}\|_{C^0(I_j)}
+\|\bar{\chi}_{\mathfrak a}-\bar{\chi}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}\|_{C^0(I_j)}
\le C\,(|\alpha|+|\beta|)\,
\|\mathfrak a-\tilde{\mathfrak a}\|_{\mathcal X}\, .
\end{equation}
In particular, the critical values satisfy $|w_{\mathfrak a}(z_j)-w_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}(z_j)|\le C(|\alpha|+|\beta|)\|\mathfrak a-\tilde{\mathfrak a}\|_{\mathcal X}$.
\item\label{it:cp-matrices} Suppose that both families $(\mathfrak a_\delta)$ and $(\tilde{\mathfrak a}_\delta)$ satisfy the first-order-system identities for every $\delta\in[0,\delta_1]$.
Then the endpoint evaluation matrices
\begin{equation}
\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta}:\
(\alpha,\beta)\longmapsto(w,\bar{\chi})(z_j\pm\varsigma)
\end{equation}
satisfy
\begin{equation}
\|\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta}\|
+\|(\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta})^{-1}\|\le C,
\qquad
\|\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta}-\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a},\delta}\|
+\|(\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta})^{-1}
-(\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a},\delta})^{-1}\|
\le C\,\|\mathfrak a-\tilde{\mathfrak a}\|_{\mathcal X}\, .
\end{equation}
\item\label{it:cp-transfer} Under the assumption of \ref{it:cp-matrices}, the maps $T=\mathcal E^{-}\circ(\mathcal E^{+})^{-1}$ of \cref{prop:convergence}, which carry the values at $z_j+\varsigma$ to the values at $z_j-\varsigma$, and their inverses are bounded and satisfy the same Lipschitz bound in $\mathfrak a$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
For \ref{it:cp-fixed}, let $\mathcal B_{\mathfrak a}$ denote the map of \cref{lem:fixedpoint} built from $\mathfrak a$, and set $w:=w_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}$.
The uniform $\tfrac12$-contraction gives $\|w_{\mathfrak a}-w\|_\infty \le2\,\|\mathcal B_{\mathfrak a}[w] -\mathcal B_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}[w]\|_\infty$, and $\|w\|_\infty\le C(|\alpha|+|\beta|)$ by \cref{lem:fixedpoint}.
We compare the terms of the two maps applied to this $w$.
The functions $E$ and $\tilde E$ computed from $b$ and $\tilde b$ obey $\|E-\tilde E\|_{C^1}\le C\|b-\tilde b\|_{C^0}$.
The functions $\bar{\chi}_{\mathfrak a}[w]$ and $\bar{\chi}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}[w]$ defined by Equation~\eqref{eq:rep-chi} take the same value $\beta$ at $z_j$ and obey $\|\bar{\chi}_{\mathfrak a}[w]-\bar{\chi}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}[w]\|_\infty +\mathrm{Lip}\bigl(\bar{\chi}_{\mathfrak a}[w] -\bar{\chi}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}[w]\bigr) \le C\,(|\beta|+\|w\|_\infty)\, \|\mathfrak a-\tilde{\mathfrak a}\|_{\mathcal X}$.
The regular terms of $\mathcal B$, namely the prefactor $\alpha VE$, the term $-Ef\bar{\chi}/U'$, and the $g_2$-integral, therefore contribute $C(|\alpha|+|\beta|)\|\mathfrak a-\tilde{\mathfrak a}\|_{\mathcal X}$.
A singular integral $V\int_{z_j+\varsigma}^{z}\varphi/V\,\,\mathrm{d} s$ with a Lipschitz function $\varphi$ is bounded by $C\|\varphi\|_{C^{0,1}}$, by the decomposition in Equation~\eqref{eq:logextract} and $\sup_{I_j}|V|\bigl(1+\bigl|\log|V|\bigr|\bigr) \le C\varsigma\log(1/\varsigma)$.
The singular terms of the two maps differ by $V(E-\tilde E)\int g_1\bar{\chi}_{\mathfrak a}[w]/V$ and $V\tilde E\int\psi/V$, with the difference $\psi:=g_1\bar{\chi}_{\mathfrak a}[w] -\tilde g_1\bar{\chi}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}[w]$.
The first is bounded by $C\|b-\tilde b\|_{C^0}(|\alpha|+|\beta|)$.
The function $\psi$ is Lipschitz with $\|\psi\|_{C^{0,1}}\le C(|\alpha|+|\beta|) \|\mathfrak a-\tilde{\mathfrak a}\|_{\mathcal X}$, and its value at the critical level is $\psi(z_j)=(g_1-\tilde g_1)(z_j)\,\beta$, so the second is bounded by the same quantity.
This proves the bound for $w_{\mathfrak a}-w_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}$.
A linear map between normed spaces is bounded if the norm of the image of each element is at most a constant times the norm of the element, and a bounded linear functional is a bounded linear map into $\mathbb{C}$.
The bound for $\bar{\chi}_{\mathfrak a}-\bar{\chi}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}$ follows, because the map $w\mapsto\bar{\chi}_{\mathfrak a}[w]$ is affine with a bounded linear part and the functions $\bar{\chi}_{\mathfrak a}[w]$ and $\bar{\chi}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a}}[w]$ obey the estimate above.
Evaluation at $z_j$ is a bounded linear functional on $C^0(I_j)$, which gives the bound for the critical values.
At $\delta=0$, the explicit formula in Equation~\eqref{eq:wc-beta} exhibits the same Lipschitz dependence through $(f,\bar{\rho}_0)(z_j)$.

For \ref{it:cp-matrices}, the columns of $\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta}$ are the values at $z_j\pm\varsigma$ of the fixed points with $(\alpha,\beta)=(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$.
They are bounded by \cref{lem:fixedpoint} and Lipschitz in $\mathfrak a$ by \ref{it:cp-fixed}, so $\|\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta}\|\le C$ and $\|\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta} -\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\tilde{\mathfrak a},\delta}\|\le C\|\mathfrak a-\tilde{\mathfrak a}\|_{\mathcal X}$.
For tuples satisfying the first-order-system identities, the fixed points solve the system in Equation~\eqref{eq:wchi-recall} by \S\ref{subsec:B-equiv}, and the identity $\Xi'=(V'/V)\Xi$ for the modified Wronskian of \cref{prop:local}\ref{it:basis-wronskian} applies.
The proof of \cref{prop:convergence}\ref{it:conv-matrices}, with constants that depend only on the bounds of \S\ref{subsec:local}, then gives $\det\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta} =\varkappa_{\mathfrak a,\delta}V_\delta(z_j\pm\varsigma)$ with $|\varkappa_{\mathfrak a,\delta}|\ge\tfrac12$.
Hence $|\det\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta}|\ge\tfrac12m_U\varsigma$, and the formula for the inverse of a $2\times2$ matrix gives $\|(\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta})^{-1}\| \le C\|\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta}\|/|\det\mathcal E^{\pm}_{\mathfrak a,\delta}|\le C$.
The Lipschitz bound for the inverses follows from the identity $\mathcal E^{-1}-\tilde{\mathcal E}^{-1} =\mathcal E^{-1}(\tilde{\mathcal E}-\mathcal E)\tilde{\mathcal E}^{-1}$.

For \ref{it:cp-transfer}, the maps $T=\mathcal E^{-}\circ(\mathcal E^{+})^{-1}$ and $T^{-1}=\mathcal E^{+}\circ(\mathcal E^{-})^{-1}$ are products of matrices that are bounded and Lipschitz in $\mathfrak a$ by \ref{it:cp-matrices}, so they have both properties as well.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:pullback}
Let $K$ be a compact set of regular values of $U$ with the same number of critical levels, and pull each critical neighborhood back to the common interval $[-\varsigma,\varsigma]$ by $z=z_j(c_r)+x$.
Assume that the coefficient tuple $\mathfrak a=(b,\bar{\rho}_0,f,g_1,g_2)$ is, in the pulled-back variables, $C^1$ in $c_r$ with values in $C^1([-\varsigma,\varsigma])$, and that it ranges in a bounded set on which the map $\mathcal B$ of \cref{lem:fixedpoint} is a $\tfrac12$-contraction.
Then the following hold uniformly for $\delta\in[0,\delta_1]$ and $c_r\in K$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item\label{it:pb-map} The pulled-back map $\mathcal B$ is $C^1$ in $c_r$ as a map into $C^0([-\varsigma,\varsigma])$, with uniformly bounded derivative.
\item\label{it:pb-fixed} The pulled-back fixed points, as maps into $C^0([-\varsigma,\varsigma])$, the endpoint evaluation matrices $\mathcal E^{\pm}$, and the critical values $w(z_j(c_r))$ are $C^1$ in $c_r$ with uniformly bounded derivatives.
\item\label{it:pb-inverse} If, in addition, the tuple satisfies the first-order-system identities for every $c_r\in K$ and every $\delta\in[0,\delta_1]$, then the endpoint matrices are uniformly invertible, and their inverses and the maps $T=\mathcal E^{-}(\mathcal E^{+})^{-1}$ are $C^1$ in $c_r$ with uniformly bounded derivatives.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
Set $I:=[-\varsigma,\varsigma]$ and write $z=z_j(c_r)+x$ with $x\in I$.
A tilde marks a pullback, $\tilde\varphi(x,c_r):=\varphi(z_j(c_r)+x)$.
Every bound is uniform in $(c_r,\delta)\in K\times[0,\delta_1]$.
Differentiating $U(z_j(c_r))=c_r$ gives $z_j'(c_r)=1/U'(z_j(c_r))$.

In the variable $x$, the interval, the base point, and both endpoints do not move.
Only the functions of $x$ in the formulas below depend on $c_r$.
We write $\tilde\tau(x,c_r):=U(z_j(c_r)+x)-c_r$, $\tilde V:=\tilde\tau-\mathrm{i}\delta$, and $\tilde B(x):=\int_0^x\tilde b$, and we denote by $\tilde\chi[w]$ the pullback of the map $w\mapsto\bar{\chi}[w]$ of the proof of \cref{lem:fixedpoint} and by $\mathcal B_{c_r,\delta}$ the pullback of the map $\mathcal B$.
On the common space $C^0(I)$, these maps read
\begin{align*}
\tilde\chi[w](x)&=e^{-\tilde B(x)}\beta
+\int_0^x e^{\tilde B(x')-\tilde B(x)}\,
\tilde\bar{\rho}_0\,k^2\,\tilde V(x')\,w(x')\,\,\mathrm{d} x',\\
\mathcal B_{c_r,\delta}[w](x)&=\alpha\,\tilde V\tilde E
-\frac{\tilde E\tilde f\,\tilde\chi[w]}{\tilde U'}
+\tilde V(x)\tilde E(x)\int_{\varsigma}^{x}
\Bigl[\frac{\tilde g_1\tilde\chi[w]}{\tilde V}
+\tilde g_2\,w\Bigr]\,\mathrm{d} x' .
\end{align*}

For \ref{it:pb-map}, let $w\in C^0(I)$ and set $\psi:=\tilde g_1\tilde\chi[w]$.
When we change variables in Equation~\eqref{eq:logextract} back to $x$ through $t=\tilde\tau(x',c_r)$ and $\,\mathrm{d} t=\tilde U'\,\mathrm{d} x'$, the singular integral splits as
\begin{equation}
\int_\varsigma^x\frac{\psi(x')}{\tilde V(x')}\,\,\mathrm{d} x'
=\frac{\psi(0)}{\tilde U'(0)}
\Bigl[\operatorname{Log}\bigl(\tilde\tau(x)-\mathrm{i}\delta\bigr)
-\operatorname{Log}\bigl(\tilde\tau(\varsigma)-\mathrm{i}\delta\bigr)\Bigr]
+\int_\varsigma^x\frac{\mathfrak h(x',c_r)}{\tilde V(x')}\,\,\mathrm{d} x',
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}
\mathfrak h(x',c_r):=\psi(x')-\psi(0)\,
\frac{\tilde U'(x')}{\tilde U'(0)} .
\end{equation}
Then $\mathfrak h(0,c_r)=0$, and $|\mathfrak h(x')|\le C\,|x'|\,\bigl(|\beta|+\|w\|_\infty\bigr)$ because $\psi$ and $\tilde U'$ are Lipschitz, with $\mathrm{Lip}(\psi)\le C(|\beta|+\|w\|_\infty)$ by Equation~\eqref{eq:chi-of-w}.

Set $\theta(x,c_r):=\tilde U'(x)/\tilde U'(0)-1$.
Then $\partial_{c_r}\tilde\tau=\theta$, $\theta(0,c_r)=0$, $|\theta(x)|\le C|x|$, and $\theta$ is uniformly Lipschitz in $x$.
Moreover, $\partial_{c_r}\tilde V=\theta$ for every $\delta$.
The pulled-back coefficients, $\tilde E$, and $e^{\pm\tilde B}$ are $C^1$ in $c_r$ with the stated function-space regularity.
Differentiating the formula for $\tilde\chi[w]$ above, whose integrand has no singularity, shows that $\tilde\chi[w]$ is $C^1$ in $c_r$ and that $\partial_{c_r}\tilde\chi[w]$ is Lipschitz in $x$, with norm bounded by $C(|\beta|+\|w\|_\infty)$.
Consequently $\partial_{c_r}\psi$ is Lipschitz in $x$, and $\psi(0)/\tilde U'(0)=\tilde g_1(0)\beta/\tilde U'(0)$ is $C^1$ in $c_r$.

For the logarithmic term, we have, for every $\delta\in[0,\delta_1]$ and $x\neq0$,
\begin{equation}
\partial_{c_r}\bigl[\tilde V
\operatorname{Log}(\tilde\tau-\mathrm{i}\delta)\bigr]
=\theta\,\bigl[\operatorname{Log}(\tilde\tau-\mathrm{i}\delta)+1\bigr],
\qquad
\bigl|\theta\,[\operatorname{Log}(\tilde\tau-\mathrm{i}\delta)+1]\bigr|
\le C|x|\Bigl(1+\bigl|\log(m_U|x|/V_\ast)\bigr|\Bigr),
\end{equation}
which extends continuously by $0$ across $x=0$, uniformly in $\delta$.
Differentiation in $c_r$ at fixed $x$ thus produces the factor $\theta=O(x)$, which cancels the logarithmic singularity.
